  "meta_commentary": "Execution order matters: Phase A (Python) gates everything else because both the PyInstaller bundle (Phase B) and the Tauri Rust bridge (Phase C) depend on the new `dataclaw/auth.py`, the `dataclaw hf \u2026` verbs, AND the new `--json` flags on `status`/`config`. If you skip ahead to Tauri, the dashboard will crash on argparse the first time it calls `dataclaw status --json`. Equally critical: in Step 14 (`hf.rs`) DO NOT route token saves through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 Tauri v2's `tauri_plugin_shell::Command::spawn` does not wire stdin in this codepath, so the Rust side writes BOTH the Keychain entry AND `~/.cache/huggingface/token` (mode 0o600) directly via the `keyring` crate plus `std::fs`. The Python `hf login` is the headless equivalent for CLI-only users. Use `tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt` (NOT v1's `tauri::api::process::Command::new_sidecar`). PyInstaller hidden imports must include `keyring.backends.macOS`, `keyring.backends.SecretService`, `keyring.backends.fail`, `secretstorage`, `jeepney`, plus `collect_submodules(\"keyring\")` \u2014 omissions only manifest at runtime in the bundled binary, so the Phase B smoke test (`dataclaw-<triple> hf whoami --check-keyring-only`) is the cheapest gate. Per-arch sidecar (`dataclaw-aarch64-apple-darwin`, `dataclaw-x86_64-apple-darwin`) via CI matrix \u2014 NOT a true universal2 fat binary; this matches Tauri's `externalBin` triple-resolution convention. Phase 11 work is OUT of scope: build unsigned (`APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=\"\"`), no `tauri-plugin-updater`, no notarization, no signing. Tray icon is mentioned in design doc Step 28 but is \"info\"-priority \u2014 ship the single-window app first; if you add the tray, omit \"Check for updates\" because that needs the updater plugin. Config tab is read-only this phase; edit forms are deferred. The existing repo has 8 modified files + net-new Python modules (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py) that are unrelated Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work \u2014 DO NOT touch them. The CLI is already 1638 lines; the watch-items debt note flags CLI growth \u2014 keep your additions tight. When you add `--json` to `status` and `config`, reuse the existing `emit_json` helper and the `---DATACLAW_JSON---` envelope marker so the Rust `parse_json_block` works uniformly. For `hf login`, write a `FakeKeyring` test shim (in-memory dict) \u2014 never touch the real OS Keychain in Python tests. Keychain Rust round-trip test is `#[ignore]`-marked because GitHub Actions runners have a locked Keychain. Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty. }

  "baseline_test_command": "pytest --tb=no -q --no-header",
  "baseline_test_failures": [],
  "baseline_test_note": "Baseline pytest was NOT executed during finalize (permission to run pytest in this harness was denied). Executor MUST run `pytest -q` before any code changes and record the failing tests so post-change failures can be cleanly attributed to this work versus pre-existing breakage. Existing repo state already shows uncommitted edits to dataclaw/cli.py, dataclaw/config.py, dataclaw/parser.py, tests/, plus net-new files (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py, docs/expansion-design.md, etc.) \u2014 those are Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work in progress and are NOT part of this plan; do not modify them beyond the targeted edits described below.",
  "meta_commentary": "Execution order matters: Phase A (Python) gates everything else because both the PyInstaller bundle (Phase B) and the Tauri Rust bridge (Phase C) depend on the new `dataclaw/auth.py`, the `dataclaw hf \u2026` verbs, AND the new `--json` flags on `status`/`config`. If you skip ahead to Tauri, the dashboard will crash on argparse the first time it calls `dataclaw status --json`. Equally critical: in Step 14 (`hf.rs`) DO NOT route token saves through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 Tauri v2's `tauri_plugin_shell::Command::spawn` does not wire stdin in this codepath, so the Rust side writes BOTH the Keychain entry AND `~/.cache/huggingface/token` (mode 0o600) directly via the `keyring` crate plus `std::fs`. The Python `hf login` is the headless equivalent for CLI-only users. Use `tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt` (NOT v1's `tauri::api::process::Command::new_sidecar`). PyInstaller hidden imports must include `keyring.backends.macOS`, `keyring.backends.SecretService`, `keyring.backends.fail`, `secretstorage`, `jeepney`, plus `collect_submodules(\"keyring\")` \u2014 omissions only manifest at runtime in the bundled binary, so the Phase B smoke test (`dataclaw-<triple> hf whoami --check-keyring-only`) is the cheapest gate. Per-arch sidecar (`dataclaw-aarch64-apple-darwin`, `dataclaw-x86_64-apple-darwin`) via CI matrix \u2014 NOT a true universal2 fat binary; this matches Tauri's `externalBin` triple-resolution convention. Phase 11 work is OUT of scope: build unsigned (`APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=\"\"`), no `tauri-plugin-updater`, no notarization, no signing. Tray icon is mentioned in design doc Step 28 but is \"info\"-priority \u2014 ship the single-window app first; if you add the tray, omit \"Check for updates\" because that needs the updater plugin. Config tab is read-only this phase; edit forms are deferred. The existing repo has 8 modified files + net-new Python modules (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py) that are unrelated Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work \u2014 DO NOT touch them. The CLI is already 1638 lines; the watch-items debt note flags CLI growth \u2014 keep your additions tight. For `hf login`, write a `FakeKeyring` test shim (in-memory dict) \u2014 never touch the real OS Keychain in Python tests. If you discover the universal2 question matters mid-execution, stop and flag \u2014 switching from per-arch to universal2 changes the build script meaningfully. Open questions from the plan (universal2 vs per-arch, tray icon scope, Config edit forms, ignored Keychain test in CI) \u2014 proceed with the assumed answers (per-arch, no tray this phase, read-only Config, local-only ignored test) but call them out in the PR description.",

Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`.       "id": "T24",
      "description": "Final verification gate. Run the project's existing test suites: (1) `pytest -q` \u2014 if any test fails, read the error, fix the underlying issue, re-run until clean. Pay special attention to the new tests added in T5; do NOT add new test files beyond those. (2) `cargo test --manifest-path app/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` \u2014 must pass `parse_json_block_*`, `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600`, `delete_mirror_removes_file`, capabilities file shape test, log_dir test. The `hf_round_trip` test must remain `#[ignore]`-marked. (3) `bash scripts/build-sidecar.sh` on the host arch \u2014 must produce an executable at `app/src-tauri/binaries/dataclaw-<host-triple>` and the smoke `hf whoami --check-keyring-only` must complete without a Python traceback (non-zero exit when no token configured is fine; a traceback means a hidden import is missing). Use tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt (Tauri v2 API). PyInstaller universal2 binary as externalBin sidecar. macOS Keychain via keyring Cargo crate. First-run modal, Run Now button, Logs tab with notify crate file watcher. Add Rust unit tests for the CLI bridge.

Existing repo state already shows uncommitted edits to dataclaw/cli.py, dataclaw/config.py, dataclaw/parser.py, tests/, plus net-new files (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py, docs/expansion-design.md, etc.) \u2014 those are Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work in progress and are NOT part of this plan; do not modify them beyond the targeted edits described below.",
  "meta_commentary": "Execution order matters: Phase A (Python) gates everything else because both the PyInstaller bundle (Phase B) and the Tauri Rust bridge (Phase C) depend on the new `dataclaw/auth.py`, the `dataclaw hf \u2026` verbs, AND the new `--json` flags on `status`/`config`. If you skip ahead to Tauri, the dashboard will crash on argparse the first time it calls `dataclaw status --json`. Equally critical: in Step 14 (`hf.rs`) DO NOT route token saves through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 Tauri v2's `tauri_plugin_shell::Command::spawn` does not wire stdin in this codepath, so the Rust side writes BOTH the Keychain entry AND `~/.cache/huggingface/token` (mode 0o600) directly via the `keyring` crate plus `std::fs`. The Python `hf login` is the headless equivalent for CLI-only users. Use `tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt` (NOT v1's `tauri::api::process::Command::new_sidecar`). PyInstaller hidden imports must include `keyring.backends.macOS`, `keyring.backends.SecretService`, `keyring.backends.fail`, `secretstorage`, `jeepney`, plus `collect_submodules(\"keyring\")` \u2014 omissions only manifest at runtime in the bundled binary, so the Phase B smoke test (`dataclaw-<triple> hf whoami --check-keyring-only`) is the cheapest gate. Per-arch sidecar (`dataclaw-aarch64-apple-darwin`, `dataclaw-x86_64-apple-darwin`) via CI matrix \u2014 NOT a true universal2 fat binary; this matches Tauri's `externalBin` triple-resolution convention. Phase 11 work is OUT of scope: build unsigned (`APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=\"\"`), no `tauri-plugin-updater`, no notarization, no signing. Tray icon is mentioned in design doc Step 28 but is \"info\"-priority \u2014 ship the single-window app first; if you add the tray, omit \"Check for updates\" because that needs the updater plugin. Config tab is read-only this phase; edit forms are deferred. The existing repo has 8 modified files + net-new Python modules (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py) that are unrelated Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work \u2014 DO NOT touch them. The CLI is already 1638 lines; the watch-items debt note flags CLI growth \u2014 keep your additions tight. When you add `--json` to `status` and `config`, reuse the existing `emit_json` helper and the `---DATACLAW_JSON---` envelope marker so the Rust `parse_json_block` works uniformly. For `hf login`, write a `FakeKeyring` test shim (in-memory dict) \u2014 never touch the real OS Keychain in Python tests. Keychain Rust round-trip test is `#[ignore]`-marked because GitHub Actions runners have a locked Keychain. If you discover the universal2 question matters mid-execution, stop and flag \u2014 switching from per-arch to universal2 changes the build script meaningfully. Open questions from the plan (universal2 vs per-arch, tray icon scope, Config edit forms, ignored Keychain test in CI) \u2014 proceed with the assumed answers (per-arch, no tray this phase, read-only Config, local-only ignored test) but call them out in the PR description.",
  "watch_items": [
    "FLAG-001 resolved by direct Rust-side dual-write in `hf.rs` \u2014 the Rust `hf_save_token` MUST write both Keychain AND `~/.cache/huggingface/token` (chmod 0o600 on Unix) without subprocess round-trip; do not regress to `hf login --token-stdin` because Tauri's spawn API does not wire stdin in this code path.",
    "FLAG-002 resolved by adding `--json` flags to `status` and `config`; the Rust bridge calls `dataclaw status --json` / `dataclaw config --json` and would crash on argparse without these flags. Verify with `test_status_json_emits_envelope_block` and `test_config_json_respects_show_secrets` before touching Tauri.",
    "PyInstaller hidden imports for keyring backends (`keyring.backends.macOS`, `keyring.backends.SecretService`, `keyring.backends.fail`, `secretstorage`, `jeepney`) \u2014 omissions only manifest at runtime in the bundled binary. Run `dataclaw-<triple> hf whoami --check-keyring-only` against the bundled binary to gate.",
    "Tauri v2 capability schema \u2014 use `shell:allow-spawn` with `{ name: \"dataclaw\", sidecar: true, args: [{validator: \".*\"}] }`; v1-style allowlist will fail JSON validation at build time.",
    "Use `tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt` (v2) \u2014 NEVER v1's `tauri::api::process::Command::new_sidecar`. The plan's success criteria explicitly check this.",
    "Per-arch sidecar (`dataclaw-aarch64-apple-darwin`, `dataclaw-x86_64-apple-darwin`) via CI matrix \u2014 NOT universal2 fat binary. Tauri externalBin auto-resolves the triple suffix.",
    "APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=\"\" \u2014 Phase 11 release infra (signing, notarization, updater plugin, latest.json) is OUT of scope here. Do not add `tauri-plugin-updater`.",
    "Token mirror path must match exactly between Python `_store_hf_token` and Rust `write_mirror` \u2014 both `~/.cache/huggingface/token` with mode 0o600. Drift here causes silent two-source-of-truth bugs.",
    "Existing repo has uncommitted edits to dataclaw/cli.py, dataclaw/config.py, dataclaw/parser.py, tests, plus net-new privacy_filter.py/scheduler.py/logging.py. Those are Phase 1\u20135/8/10 \u2014 DO NOT modify them.",
    "Watch-items debt: dataclaw/cli.py is already 1638 lines. New `hf` subparser + `--json` flags add ~150 lines; keep additions surgical, no opportunistic refactors.",
    "Watch-items debt: tauri.conf.json updater public key NOT planned (out of scope). If you accidentally include `tauri-plugin-updater` it will demand a public key and fail.",
    "FakeKeyring test shim \u2014 never touch real OS Keychain in Python tests. Pattern: `monkeypatch.setattr(\"dataclaw.auth.keyring\", FakeKeyring())`.",
    "Rust Keychain round-trip test must be `#[ignore]`-marked \u2014 GitHub Actions runners have a locked Keychain by default.",
    "Atomic mirror write order in Rust: write Keychain FIRST, then `write_mirror`. If mirror write fails, the Keychain still has the token and `_resolve_hf_token` will find it, so the user is not locked out.",
    "First-run modal logic: invoke `hf_load_token` AND `dataclaw_config_get` on App mount; only block when token is null AND config.repo is set (not when repo is unset \u2014 that's a fresh install with no work to do).",
    "Logs.tsx ring buffer cap \u2264 5000 lines \u2014 per success criterion. Long-running streams MUST NOT grow unbounded.",
    "Open question (universal2 vs per-arch) \u2014 assumed answer: per-arch matrix. If user wants universal2, build script needs `lipo` post-merge step.",
    "Open question (tray icon scope) \u2014 assumed answer: no tray this phase (omit `TrayIconBuilder`). If added, do NOT include 'Check for updates' menu item (needs updater plugin which is Phase 11).",
    "Open question (Config edit forms) \u2014 assumed answer: read-only first cut. Edit forms tracked as follow-up.",
    "Open question (ignored Keychain test in CI) \u2014 assumed answer: local-only.",
    "Reuse existing `emit_json` helper and `---DATACLAW_JSON---` envelope marker \u2014 do NOT invent a new format for `--json` output."

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty. [\"--force\"]:[]))`; `dataclaw_config_get(show_secrets: bool)` \u2192 `run_sidecar(&app, &[\"config\", \"--json\"]+(show_secrets? [\"--show-secrets\"]:[]), None)`.       "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/hf.rs` (NEW): the Keychain bridge that writes BOTH backends directly in Rust \u2014 NO subprocess round-trip. Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. Add the `#[ignore]`-marked `hf_round_trip` test that exercises set/get/delete on a throw-away service `io.dataclaw.test`. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty. invoke } from \"@tauri-apps/api/core\";\n+import { listen } from \"@tauri-apps/api/event\";\n+import { homeDir } from \"@tauri-apps/api/path\";\n+import { readTextFile } from \"@tauri-apps/plugin-fs\";\n+import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from \"react\";\n+\n+const MAX_LINES = 5000;\n+\n+type LogRow = {\n+  raw: string;\n+  level: string;\n+  message: string;\n+};\n+\n+function todayLogName() {\n+  return `auto-${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}.jsonl`;\n+}\n+\n+function parseLine(raw: string): LogRow {\n+  try {\n+    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { level?: unknown; message?: unknown; msg?: unknown };\n+    const level = typeof parsed.level === \"string\" ? parsed.level.toUpperCase() : \"INFO\";\n+    const messageValue = parsed.message ?? parsed.msg;\n+    return {\n+      raw,\n+      level,\n+      message: typeof messageValue === \"string\" ? messageValue : raw\n+    };\n+  } catch {\n+    return { raw, level: \"INFO\", message: raw };\n+  }\n+}\n+\n+function appendBounded(current: string[], next: string[]) {\n+  return [...current, ...next].slice(-MAX_LINES);\n+}\n+\n+function rowColor(level: string) {\n+  if (level === \"DEBUG\") return \"#6b7280\";\n+  if (level === \"WARNING\" || level === \"WARN\") return \"#b45309\";\n+  if (level === \"ERROR\") return \"#b91c1c\";\n+  return \"#1f2937\";\n+}\n+\n+export default function Logs() {\n+  const [lines, setLines] = useState<string[]>([]);\n+  const [filter, setFilter] = useState(\"\");\n+  const [copyState, setCopyState] = useState(\"\");\n+\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    let disposed = false;\n+    let unlisten: (() => void) | undefined;\n+\n+    async function loadInitialLines() {\n+      try {\n+        const home = await homeDir();\n+        const text = await readTextFile(`${home}.dataclaw/logs/${todayLogName()}`);\n+        if (!disposed) {\n+          setLines(text.split(/\\r?\\n/).filter(Boolean).slice(-200));\n+        }\n+      } catch {\n+        if (!disposed) {\n+          setLines([]);\n+        }\n+      }\n+    }\n+\n+    async function subscribe() {\n+      unlisten = await listen<string>(\"logs-line\", (event) => {\n+        setLines((current) => appendBounded(current, [event.payload]));\n+      });\n+    }\n+\n+    void loadInitialLines();\n+    void subscribe();\n+\n+    return () => {\n+      disposed = true;\n+      if (unlisten) {\n+        unlisten();\n+      }\n+    };\n+  }, []);\n+\n+  const rows = useMemo(() => lines.map(parseLine), [lines]);\n+  const visibleRows = useMemo(() => {\n+    const q = filter.trim().toLowerCase();\n+    if (!q) return rows;\n+    return rows.filter((row) => row.raw.toLowerCase().includes(q));\n+  }, [filter, rows]);\n+\n+  async function copyVisible() {\n+    await navigator.clipboard.writeText(visibleRows.map((row) => row.raw).join(\"\\n\"));\n+    setCopyState(\"Copied\");\n+    window.setTimeout(() => setCopyState(\"\"), 1200);\n+  }\n+\n+  return (\n+    <section style={{ padding: 16, fontFamily: \"system-ui, sans-serif\" }}>\n+      <header style={{ display: \"flex\", gap: 8, alignItems: \"center\", marginBottom: 12 }}>\n+        <input\n+          aria-label=\"Filter logs\"\n+          value={filter}\n+          onChange={(event) => setFilter(event.target.value)}\n+          placeholder=\"Filter\"\n+          style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 0, padding: \"8px 10px\" }}\n+        />\n+        <button type=\"button\" onClick={() => void invoke(\"logs_open_in_finder\")}>\n+          Show in Finder\n+        </button>\n+        <button type=\"button\" onClick={() => void copyVisible()}>\n+          Copy all visible\n+        </button>\n+      </header>\n+      {copyState ? <p style={{ margin: \"0 0 8px\" }}>{copyState}</p> : null}\n+      <div\n+        style={{\n+          border: \"1px solid #d1d5db\",\n+          borderRadius: 6,\n+          height: \"calc(100vh - 140px)\",\n+          minHeight: 360,\n+          overflow: \"auto\",\n+          background: \"#ffffff\"\n+        }}\n+      >\n+        {visibleRows.map((row, index) => (\n+          <div\n+            key={`${index}-${row.raw.slice(0, 32)}`}\n+            style={{\n+              display: \"grid\",\n+              gridTemplateColumns: \"88px minmax(0, 1fr)\",\n+              gap: 8,\n+              padding: \"6px 8px\",\n+              borderBottom: \"1px solid #f3f4f6\",\n+              color: rowColor(row.level),\n+              fontFamily: \"ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace\",\n+              fontSize: 12,\n+              lineHeight: 1.4\n+            }}\n+          >\n+            <strong>{row.level}</strong>\n+            <span style={{ whiteSpace: \"pre-wrap\", overflowWrap: \"anywhere\" }}>{row.message}</span>\n+          </div>\n+        ))}\n+      </div>\n+    </section>\n+  );\n+}\n*** End Patch\n"
}

      "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/hf.rs` (NEW): the Keychain bridge that writes BOTH backends directly in Rust \u2014 NO subprocess round-trip. Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. Add the `#[ignore]`-marked `hf_round_trip` test that exercises set/get/delete on a throw-away service `io.dataclaw.test`. CRITICAL: do NOT route through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 the previous draft did and would have left the mirror unwritten because Tauri's spawn API doesn't wire stdin in this path.",

Executor MUST run `pytest -q` before any code changes and record the failing tests so post-change failures can be cleanly attributed to this work versus pre-existing breakage. Existing repo state already shows uncommitted edits to dataclaw/cli.py, dataclaw/config.py, dataclaw/parser.py, tests/, plus net-new files (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py, docs/expansion-design.md, etc.) \u2014 those are Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work in progress and are NOT part of this plan; do not modify them beyond the targeted edits described below.",
  "meta_commentary": "Execution order matters: Phase A (Python) gates everything else because both the PyInstaller bundle (Phase B) and the Tauri Rust bridge (Phase C) depend on the new `dataclaw/auth.py`, the `dataclaw hf \u2026` verbs, AND the new `--json` flags on `status`/`config`. If you skip ahead to Tauri, the dashboard will crash on argparse the first time it calls `dataclaw status --json`. Equally critical: in Step 14 (`hf.rs`) DO NOT route token saves through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 Tauri v2's `tauri_plugin_shell::Command::spawn` does not wire stdin in this codepath, so the Rust side writes BOTH the Keychain entry AND `~/.cache/huggingface/token` (mode 0o600) directly via the `keyring` crate plus `std::fs`. The Python `hf login` is the headless equivalent for CLI-only users. Use `tauri_plugin_shell::ShellExt` (NOT v1's `tauri::api::process::Command::new_sidecar`). PyInstaller hidden imports must include `keyring.backends.macOS`, `keyring.backends.SecretService`, `keyring.backends.fail`, `secretstorage`, `jeepney`, plus `collect_submodules(\"keyring\")` \u2014 omissions only manifest at runtime in the bundled binary, so the Phase B smoke test (`dataclaw-<triple> hf whoami --check-keyring-only`) is the cheapest gate. Per-arch sidecar (`dataclaw-aarch64-apple-darwin`, `dataclaw-x86_64-apple-darwin`) via CI matrix \u2014 NOT a true universal2 fat binary; this matches Tauri's `externalBin` triple-resolution convention. Phase 11 work is OUT of scope: build unsigned (`APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=\"\"`), no `tauri-plugin-updater`, no notarization, no signing. Tray icon is mentioned in design doc Step 28 but is \"info\"-priority \u2014 ship the single-window app first; if you add the tray, omit \"Check for updates\" because that needs the updater plugin. Config tab is read-only this phase; edit forms are deferred. The existing repo has 8 modified files + net-new Python modules (privacy_filter.py, scheduler.py, logging.py) that are unrelated Phase 1\u20135/8/10 work \u2014 DO NOT touch them. The CLI is already 1638 lines; the watch-items debt note flags CLI growth \u2014 keep your additions tight. When you add `--json` to `status` and `config`, reuse the existing `emit_json` helper and the `---DATACLAW_JSON---` envelope marker so the Rust `parse_json_block` works uniformly. For `hf login`, write a `FakeKeyring` test shim (in-memory dict) \u2014 never touch the real OS Keychain in Python tests. Keychain Rust round-trip test is `#[ignore]`-marked because GitHub Actions runners have a locked Keychain. If you discover the universal2 question matters mid-execution, stop and flag \u2014 switching from per-arch to universal2 changes the build script meaningfully. Open questions from the plan (universal2 vs per-arch, tray icon scope, Config edit forms, ignored Keychain test in CI) \u2014 proceed with the assumed answers (per-arch, no tray this phase, read-only Config, local-only ignored test) but call them out in the PR description.",

      "id": "T14",
      "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/hf.rs` (NEW): the Keychain bridge that writes BOTH backends directly in Rust \u2014 NO subprocess round-trip. Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. Add the `#[ignore]`-marked `hf_round_trip` test that exercises set/get/delete on a throw-away service `io.dataclaw.test`. CRITICAL: do NOT route through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 the previous draft did and would have left the mirror unwritten because Tauri's spawn API doesn't wire stdin in this path.",

      "id": "T17",
      "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/logs.rs` (NEW): `notify`-driven file watcher for `~/.dataclaw/logs/`. `log_dir()` resolves `dirs::home_dir().unwrap().join(\".dataclaw/logs\")`. `start_log_watcher(app: AppHandle)` mkdirs the dir, sets up a `Mutex<HashMap<PathBuf, u64>>` to track per-file byte positions, builds a `RecommendedWatcher` whose closure: filters to `EventKind::Modify(_) | Create(_)`, filters paths to `*.jsonl`, opens the file, seeks to last position, reads new lines via `BufReader::lines`, calls `app_handle.emit(\"logs-line\", &line)` for each, updates position. Watch the dir non-recursively. Stash watcher in `app.manage(Mutex::new(watcher))` so it survives drop.         Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] blocked-auto-review-command: blocked auto review command: plan tells users to run `dataclaw confirm <run_dir>` without specifying the required argparse positional path change. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] cli-growth: cli growth: plan assigns most new command behavior to an already-large dataclaw/cli.py despite its own manageability criterion. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] code-organization: dataclaw/cli.py is 1638 lines; plan adds significant new command behavior to it. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked `/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/text-ip-adapter` and its `docs/` directory: the repository still contains only an empty `docs` folder, so the plan's own must-have deliverable `docs/megaplan.md` has not been materialized in the actual repo. that leaves the explicit output requirement and the success criterion about ending with the canonical settled decisions section unmet in repository state. - [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: compared the revised fallback/auth story across both repositories. `text-ip-adapter` now plans to document `runpod_ssh_password` in its own `env.example`, but the sibling `runpod-lifecycle` config parser and readme still do not recognize that variable. if the fallback path remains in scope, the plan has not touched all supporting configuration locations needed to make that auth story coherent across the two packages. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the blocked-auto notification and review flow repeatedly reference `dataclaw confirm <run_dir>`, but the current cli only accepts confirm targets through `--file/-f` and the plan's step 11 describes `_find_export_target(hint)` without explicitly adding a positional confirm path or changing the notification to `dataclaw confirm --file <run_dir>`. if implemented literally, the command shown to users after a blocked auto run will fail argument parsing. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. Add the `#[ignore]`-marked `hf_round_trip` test that exercises set/get/delete on a throw-away service `io.dataclaw.test`. CRITICAL: do NOT route through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 the previous draft did and would have left the mirror unwritten because Tauri's spawn API doesn't wire stdin in this path.",

Implement async `run_sidecar(app, args, env)` that calls `app.shell().sidecar(\"dataclaw\")`, applies env vars, spawns, drains `CommandEvent::Stdout` until `Terminated`, then `parse_json_block`. Add three `#[tauri::command]`s: `dataclaw_status` \u2192 `run_sidecar(&app, &[\"status\", \"--json\"], None)`; `dataclaw_auto_now(force: bool)` \u2192 `crate::hf::run_with_token(&app, &[\"auto\"]+(force? [\"--force\"]:[]))`; `dataclaw_config_get(show_secrets: bool)` \u2192 `run_sidecar(&app, &[\"config\", \"--json\"]+(show_secrets? [\"--show-secrets\"]:[]), None)`.       "id": "T14",
      "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/hf.rs` (NEW): the Keychain bridge that writes BOTH backends directly in Rust \u2014 NO subprocess round-trip. Constants `SERVICE = \"io.dataclaw.app\"`, `ACCOUNT = \"hf_token\"`. `entry()` builds a `keyring::Entry`. `hf_token_path()` resolves `~/.cache/huggingface/token` via `dirs::home_dir()`. `write_mirror(&token)`: mkdir parent, write file, then on `#[cfg(unix)]` set permissions to `0o600` via `PermissionsExt`. `delete_mirror()`: best-effort `remove_file`. `#[tauri::command] hf_save_token(token: String)` \u2192 `entry()?.set_password(&token)?` THEN `write_mirror(&token)?`. `hf_load_token()` \u2192 returns `Some/None/Err` mapping `keyring::Error::NoEntry` \u2192 `Ok(None)`. `hf_delete_token()` \u2192 best-effort delete BOTH (Keychain via `delete_credential`, file via `delete_mirror`). `run_with_token(app, args)` \u2192 `hf_load_token()` \u2192 if non-empty wrap in HashMap as `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `crate::dataclaw::run_sidecar(app, args, env)`. `hf_whoami` command \u2192 `run_with_token(&app, &[\"hf\", \"whoami\"])`. Include `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` for `write_mirror_writes_token_with_chmod_600` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, point `HOME` at temp dir, assert `(metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o777) == 0o600`) and `delete_mirror_removes_file`. Add the `#[ignore]`-marked `hf_round_trip` test that exercises set/get/delete on a throw-away service `io.dataclaw.test`. CRITICAL: do NOT route through `dataclaw hf login --token-stdin` \u2014 the previous draft did and would have left the mirror unwritten because Tauri's spawn API doesn't wire stdin in this path.",

        "T11",
        "T13"
      ],

      "description": "Author `app/src-tauri/src/logs.rs` (NEW): `notify`-driven file watcher for `~/.dataclaw/logs/`. `log_dir()` resolves `dirs::home_dir().unwrap().join(\".dataclaw/logs\")`. `start_log_watcher(app: AppHandle)` mkdirs the dir, sets up a `Mutex<HashMap<PathBuf, u64>>` to track per-file byte positions, builds a `RecommendedWatcher` whose closure: filters to `EventKind::Modify(_) | Create(_)`, filters paths to `*.jsonl`, opens the file, seeks to last position, reads new lines via `BufReader::lines`, calls `app_handle.emit(\"logs-line\", &line)` for each, updates position. Add `#[tauri::command] logs_open_in_finder()` that runs `Command::new(\"open\").arg(log_dir()).status()`. Include a `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` unit test asserting `log_dir()` ends with `.dataclaw/logs` (full notify-loop testing requires a Tauri app handle and is deferred to manual smoke).",

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

If a test fails and its ID appears in baseline_test_failures, it is pre-existing — do not scope-creep into fixing it. If baseline_test_failures is null, the baseline could not be captured; use your judgment but err on the side of assuming failures are regressions. You MUST still re-run the FULL test suite with your changes applied — pre-existing failures do not excuse skipping verification. Never narrow to individual test functions and stop.
        - If this batch includes the final verification task, write a short script that reproduces the exact bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix resolves it, then delete the script.
